===== PAGE 3 =====
iii
f = 111.2 Hz
A NOTE ON THIS DOCUMENT
Provenance
What follows is the complete distribution file as Andrew Chen assembled it in the seventy-two hours before its
release: the working record of a research program that began with one man's inheritance beneath Kansas City and
ended with a protocol reproducible anywhere the right stone could be found.
The file is organized as Chen left it — five parts, moving from the preparation of a candidate through the
construction of a chamber, the derivation of the frequencies themselves, the training of those who would guide the
work, and finally the raw research data on which every claim rests. Nothing has been added. A small number of names
in the candidate logs have been reduced to their Foundation identifiers, as Chen requested. Everything else stands as
released.
The reader should understand what this document is and is not. It is not a grimoire. It is not a manifesto. It is a
research file — deliberately dry, deliberately reproducible, deliberately stripped of the language that had surrounded
this material for seven centuries. Chen believed that the surest way to end the secrecy was to make the work boring: to
render it as tables and thresholds and calibration curves, so that no one could ever again claim it as a mystery to be
guarded. Whether he succeeded is left to the reader to judge.
— from the Foundation preface to the first public release

The Fifty-Two Week Curriculum
The preparation protocol documented here was developed by the William Masters Foundation under Nadia Volkov’s
direction. It replaced the improvised, dangerous approach of the early sittings with a staged, monitored,
fifty-two-week curriculum designed to bring a candidate from an untrained baseline to full harmonic reception
without harm.
The duration is not arbitrary. It derives from Sirach — an apocryphal text of fifty-one chapters, each approximately
four and a half minutes when read aloud, plus an implied fifty-second unit of silence. The preparation protocol is
fifty-two weeks long because the oldest template for graduated human transformation is fifty-two units long. The
Foundation set the duration empirically and then discovered, reading backward through the texts Eva Černá had
assembled, that the duration had been set two thousand two hundred years ago.
The curriculum rests on a single principle learned the hard way: the frequency cannot be rushed. Every adverse event
in the Foundation’s records followed a compression of the schedule. The protocol is slow by design. Its slowness is its
safety.
Total duration
52 weeks (from Sirach’s 51+1 structure)
Phases
5 (Orientation → Integration)
Screening
EEG baseline · cardiac profiling
Beta coherence floor
> 40% spontaneous (entry requirement)
Cardiac threshold
session terminated below 45 BPM
Integration window
72 hours minimum, post-session
Clinical oversight
Sabrina Volkov, M.D. (clinical director)
Institutional design
Nadia Volkov (consent framework)
Reading sequence
15 texts (Strahov / Charles University / Eva Černá)
WEEKS
PHASE
FOCUS
1–8

Screening, baseline EEG, cardiac profiling, first readings.
9–20

amplitude.

Consolidation. The 72-hour protocol. Clinical review.

THE READING SEQUENCE
The Texts That Make the Difference
The reading list is not devotional. Each text is assigned because it contains a specific technical element the candidate
will later need: a proportion, a posture, a discipline of attention, a fragment of the acoustic model. The sequence
matters. The Ars Notoria is read — not first, but fourth, after Sirach and 2 Esdras and the Gospel of Mary have
prepared the reader to understand what the notae actually are. It is read again last, in understanding.
01
Sirach (Ecclesiasticus)
BEN SIRA (C. 180 BCE) · HEBREW/GREEK · Strahov Premonstratensian Library
Fifty-one chapters plus an implied fifty-second silence. The template for the 52-week protocol.
02
2 Esdras (Fourth Book of Ezra)
ANONYMOUS (1ST C. CE) · LATIN · Charles University Archives
The text that broke everything open. The apocalyptic vision read as preparation.
03
The Gospel of Mary
ANONYMOUS (2ND C.) · COPTIC · Berlin Codex 8502.1
The ascent of the soul through the four powers. Peter's dismissal is the institutional failure the Foundation was built to avoid.
04
The Ars Notoria
ANONYMOUS (13TH C.) · LATIN · Strahov DG.IV.7
The core text. The notae as acoustic-geometric specifications.
05
Sefer Yetzirah
ANONYMOUS (ATTRIB. ABRAHAM) · HEBREW · Charles University MS 1892
The book of formation. The thirty-two paths and the doctrine of sounded letters.
06
The Cloud of Unknowing
ANONYMOUS (14TH C.) · MIDDLE ENGLISH · Charles University MS 3310
The apophatic discipline. How not-knowing prepares the listening mind.
07
De Harmonia Mundi
FRANCESCO GIORGI (1525) · LATIN · Strahov BF.II.14
Cathedral proportion as sounded ratio. The numerical soul of architecture.
08
The Zohar (Sifra di-Tzeniuta)
ANONYMOUS (13TH C.) · ARAMAIC · Strahov DG.IV.19
The book of concealment. The face and the counter-face.
09
Harmonices Mundi (Bk III–IV)
JOHANNES KEPLER (1619) · LATIN · Charles University MS 2890

The consonances as geometric necessity. The ear as an instrument of proof.
10
The Chymical Wedding
J.V. ANDREAE (1616) · GERMAN · Strahov BF.III.1
The seven-day preparation. The allegory read as protocol.
11
Theologia Germanica
ANONYMOUS (14TH C.) · GERMAN · Strahov BF.IV.8
The surrendered will. The emptying that precedes reception.
12
The Emerald Tablet (with glosses)
ANONYMOUS (ATTRIB. HERMES) · LATIN · Charles University MS 1901
As above, so below. The coupling principle in its oldest formulation.
13
Moreau’s Chamber Journals (I–III)
FATHER BENJAMIN MOREAU (1870S) · FRENCH/ENGLISH · William Masters Foundation Archive
The original SubTropolis chamber construction. The Specchi mirrors installed.
14
The Specchi Production Records
SPECCHI FAMILY (1340–1720) · ITALIAN/LATIN · Venetian State Archives / Eva Černá transcriptions
Three hundred eighty years of parabolic acoustic mirrors. Speculorum perfectum.
15
Andrew Chen — Reconstruction Notes
A. CHEN (PRESENT DAY) · ENGLISH · William Masters Foundation
The bridge document. How the preceding texts were read into a protocol.

THE CURRICULUM
Week by Week
Each week specifies a primary activity, a reading, and a logged threshold. The facilitator records completion before the
candidate advances. Nadia Volkov designed this curriculum with the structural attention of someone who
understood that the institutional framework is itself the first phase of the preparation.

CANDIDATE SCREENING
Entry Criteria
Not every candidate may begin. The screening criteria exist because the frequency does not distinguish between a
prepared nervous system and an unprepared one. Two measurements gate entry: a spontaneous beta-coherence
baseline, and a cardiac profile establishing the individual’s safe operating range.
EEG channels required
64 minimum
Spontaneous beta coherence
> 40% (measured at rest, eyes closed)
Resting heart rate
profiled over 7 days
Individual cardiac threshold
set above the 45 BPM floor
Contraindication: arrhythmia
absolute exclusion
Contraindication: seizure history
absolute exclusion
Minimum age
25 (frontal maturation)
Integration support
confirmed available for 72h post-session
The Cardiac Floor
During reception, heart rate drops. This is expected — it is one of the most reliable physiological markers that
coupling is occurring. But the drop must be bounded. The Foundation sets a threshold of forty-five beats per minute:
if a candidate’s rate falls below it, the session is adjusted immediately. Sabrina Volkov, as clinical director, established
this threshold from the Foundation’s monitoring data and the principle that the candidate’s subjective experience of
the session is not admissible evidence about the candidate’s safety. The monitor decides.

ADVERSE EVENT PROTOCOL
The Maryland Incident
The Maryland incident was not caused by the Foundation. It was caused by the Completion Sect — a group that
believed the engineering was sufficient, that you could build the chamber, produce the frequency, and understanding
would adapt. They had their own fragmentary specifications. They built their own chamber, using DOD
connections, imprecise mirrors — not the Specchi parabolic surfaces — without the preparation protocol, without
the consent architecture the Foundation had spent years establishing.
Three subjects — military-adjacent, unprepared — seized. One eventually regained limited speech. The other two
remained non-communicative: suspended in a state their unprepared neurology could not process, could not
integrate, could not communicate. Alive, aware, and unable to bridge the gap between their expanded perception and
the language systems that ordinary communication required.
The comparison the Foundation needed — and the argument the Maryland incident provided — was precise:
twenty-three prepared candidates with zero harm versus three unprepared subjects with seizures and communication
loss. The demonstration proved both the efficacy and the necessity of the preparation protocol. The Completion Sect
proved the cost of ignoring it.
TIER
ACTION
PROTOCOL
TIER 1
OBSERVE
Deviation from expected EEG or cardiac pattern within tolerance. Facilitator
increases monitoring frequency. Log every 30 seconds.
TIER 2
INTERVENE
Measured value approaching threshold (beta collapse, HR toward 45 BPM,
coherence spike). Facilitator reduces amplitude, speaks the grounding
sequence, prepares for termination. Second facilitator summoned.
TIER 3
TERMINATE
Any threshold crossed, or Tier-2 unresolved within 90 seconds. Amplitude to
zero. Candidate brought down through inversion sequence. Medical support
engaged. Session ends. No exceptions.
Father Crane — the Georgetown Jesuit theologian who had designed the original curriculum the Completion Sect perverted — contacted
Nadia Volkov by letter after the incident. Three words cost him everything he believed about urgency: “The Foundation is right.” The
graduated exposure, the consent architecture. It was the modern equivalent of what the medieval sources prescribed.

===== PAGE 57 =====
THE DISTRIBUTION FILE
APPENDIX · RESEARCH DATA
53
f = 111.2 Hz
Building a Chamber Anywhere
The central discovery documented in this part is also its most subversive: the chamber can be built anywhere the right
stone can be found. It requires basalt, or one of its acoustic equivalents; a set of dimensions; the Specchi parabolic
mirrors — or modern equivalents fabricated to the same curvature specifications — and instrumentation any
university laboratory already owns.
The specifications derive from Andrew Chen’s field configuration on the Reykjanes basalt of Iceland. One mirror in
Icelandic basalt equaled six in the SubTropolis limestone — the basalt’s natural resonance does eighty percent of the
work. This is why the file could be released. Once the chamber is reproducible, the secret is already gone.
Preferred substrate
basalt (volcanic extrusive)
Substrate f0 target
108–112 Hz
Iceland configuration
8 chambers in lava tubes, 1 mirror each
SubTropolis configuration
single chamber, 6 Specchi mirrors
Mirror type
parabolic, mercury-amalgam (Specchi / modern equiv.)
EEG monitoring
64-channel minimum, session-synced
Cardiac monitoring
continuous, threshold at 45 BPM
Seismograph
broadband, coupled to substrate
Ambient noise floor
< 26 dBA


===== PAGE 58 =====
THE DISTRIBUTION FILE
APPENDIX · RESEARCH DATA
54
f = 111.2 Hz
SUBSTRATE SELECTION
The Stones That Sing
The substrate is the chamber’s resonant body. When the fundamental is introduced, it is the stone that sustains it —
or fails to. The range is narrow: 108 to 112.1 Hz. Four hertz. The width of the human singing voice’s fundamental,
the narrowest interval the ear can resolve, the distance between two notes that the body hears as the same note played
on different instruments. The complete response table follows.

THE MIRROR TRADITION
The Specchi Family of Murano
Eva Černá found the connection in the Strahov Jesuit archives: correspondence between the Premonstratensians and
a Venetian glassmaking family, dated 1612 to 1641. The commissions specified mirrors — and the letters used the
term speculari ad resonantiam. Mirrors for resonance.
The family was named Specchi. The Italian word for mirrors. Active on Murano from approximately 1340 to 1720
— three hundred and eighty years of continuous mirror production. They were not the most commercially successful
family. They were the most specialized. Every mirror they produced was a commission, ordered by a specific
institution, produced to specific parameters, delivered by the family members themselves. Hand-delivered. To
Prague, to Paris, to Barcelona, and — in 1843 — to novum oppidum in terra occidentali: the new settlement in the
western land. Twenty-seven years before Father Benjamin Moreau built St. Francis Parish in the West Bottoms of
Kansas City.
The Specchi mirrors were parabolic. Curved, with curvatures specified to the quarter-inch, each designed to focus
reflected energy to a specific focal point. The surface was described as speculorum perfectum — the perfect mirror,
reflecting what the ledger called lumen invisibile. The surface treatment involved multiple stages of mercury
amalgam, polished to politura ad quintam, the fifth degree.
The builders designed the shape. The mirror-makers provided the surface. Two traditions, two families, collaborating
over centuries to produce a single instrument. A guild within a guild.
#
CITY
CC
DATE
INSTITUTION
TERM

novum oppidum in terra occidentali
Andrew confirmed the physics remotely: the parabolic reflectors increase the energy density at the convergence point by a factor of
approximately forty. That is the difference between hearing a resonance and being immersed in it. Between noticing a vibration and having
the vibration restructure your perceptual field.

MONITORING & INSTRUMENTATION
Three Channels, One Time Base
A chamber that cannot be monitored cannot be operated safely. The specification requires continuous,
session-synchronized capture on three channels: electroencephalography at no fewer than sixty-four electrodes,
continuous cardiac monitoring with the threshold alarm at 45 BPM, and a seismograph coupled to the substrate
itself. The seismograph is not incidental — it is how the operator confirms that the stone is responding as the model
predicts, in real time, independent of the candidate’s report.
EEG
64-channel min · 1 kHz sampling · session-synced
Cardiac
3-lead continuous · threshold alarm at 45 BPM
Seismograph
broadband · coupled to substrate floor
Audio reference
calibrated mic at candidate position
Synchronization
all channels to single time base
Acoustic dampening
NRC ≥ 0.85 above 400 Hz
Ambient floor
< 26 dBA, verified pre-session
Lempel-Ziv complexity
real-time tracking per Sabrina Volkov’s protocol

CHAMBER COMMISSIONING LEDGER
Every Chamber, Every Tuning Run
Each chamber in the network is commissioned through a documented sequence of tuning runs: the calibration
sittings by which a newly-built chamber is brought into agreement with the model before any candidate enters it.

Eight Chambers, Eight Harmonics
The harmonic structure is not esoteric. It is the harmonic series — the same series that defines every musical tradition
on earth, the physics of acoustic resonance that every vibrating body produces. The fundamental at 111.2 Hz. Its
octave at 222.4. Its third harmonic at 333.6. Up through the eighth at 889.6 Hz. Eight chambers. Eight harmonics.
Each chamber tuned to a single frequency, each frequency a whole-number multiple of the first.
The eighth chamber produced something no one had predicted: a sub-harmonic of 3.915 Hz — half the Schumann
Resonance. The Earth’s electromagnetic heartbeat, divided by the mirrors into its own fundamental. A vibration
below hearing but within perception, a tone the body felt with its entire water content responding. The planet’s
audible electromagnetic frequency at 7.83 Hz was itself the octave. The fundamental was below it. Eight mirrors
focused in basalt revealed it.
Fundamental (f0)
111.2 Hz
Harmonic series
f0 × n, n = 1–8
Eighth harmonic
889.6 Hz
Sub-harmonic revealed
3.915 Hz (half the Schumann at 7.83 Hz)
Schumann ratio
99.41:1 (near-perfect hundredth harmonic)
Cathedral range
108–112.1 Hz (five sites, four hertz)
Phase coherence
0.97 (basalt) vs. 0.85 (limestone)

CATHEDRAL CROSS-REFERENCE
Five Sites, Four Hertz
The derivation is confirmed against five independent medieval and geological structures whose acoustic signatures
were measured directly. The range is narrow: 108 to 112.1 Hz. Four hertz. The width of the human singing voice’s
fundamental, the narrowest interval the ear can resolve, the distance between two notes that the body hears as the
same note played on different instruments.
The grimoire tradition did not invent the frequency, it documented it. The cathedrals did not create the resonance,
they amplified it. The burial chambers did not summon the ancestors, they sat in the same geological hum the
ancestors had been listening to since the first human went underground and heard the earth singing. The stone
remembers. Stone does not burn.

Eighty to Nine Hundred Twenty Hertz

lock-point markers.

APPENDIX · RESEARCH DATA
189
f = 111.2 Hz
Those Who Guide the Work
Nadia Volkov designed the institutional framework. Sabrina Volkov, M.D., established the clinical monitoring
protocols. Between them, they built the safety system that the frequency itself does not provide. The frequency is
indifferent. The facilitator is not.
This part is the manual that trained the people who would guide the work. Its premise is that the facilitator, not the
frequency, is the safety system.
Institutional design
Nadia Volkov
Clinical protocols
Sabrina Volkov, M.D.
Preparation protocol
52 weeks (from Sirach)
Cardiac threshold
45 BPM
Integration window
72 hours (facilitator-owned)
Adverse-event tiers
3 (observe / intervene / terminate)

f = 111.2 Hz
SESSION MANAGEMENT
The Shape of a Session
Every session follows the same shape: a pre-session baseline, a monitored ascent through the harmonics, a sustained
plateau, and a documented descent. The facilitator’s task is to hold this shape and to break it instantly if any
monitored value demands it.
STAGE
TIMING
PROTOCOL
PRE-SESSION
−60 to 0 min
EEG baseline confirmed. Cardiac profile reviewed. Chamber verified:
substrate temperature, mirror alignment, ambient floor below 26 dBA.
Candidate posture calibrated.
ASCENT
0 to ~90 min
Fundamental introduced at −18 dB. Harmonics added in sequence, each
confirmed by coherence gain before the next. Descent path confirmed
available.
PLATEAU
sustained
Full harmonic stack sustained. Continuous monitoring. Facilitator logs
every five minutes. Lempel-Ziv complexity tracked in real time.
DESCENT
~30 min
Candidate brought down through the harmonics in reverse. Amplitude
reduced in documented steps. Return to baseline confirmed on EEG.
INTEGRATION
72 hours
Facilitator-owned. Check-ins at 1h, 6h, 24h, 72h. Session not closed
until the 72-hour follow-up is clear.


===== PAGE 195 =====
THE DISTRIBUTION FILE
APPENDIX · RESEARCH DATA

f = 111.2 Hz
THE GRASSROOTS NETWORK
How the Work Spread
By the events of the trilogy’s final volume, the work had spread in a way no institution could have organized. The
replication sites were the first: Kyoto, Buenos Aires, Lagos. But the grassroots spread was wider and stranger than any
laboratory network.
In Lagos, Adunni Okafor — a grandmother, seventy-one, no academic credentials, no institutional affiliation —
gathered eight women from her church in a concrete living room with a tin roof and a ceiling fan wobbling at 44 Hz.
Her granddaughter, a medical student, had downloaded the archive and called her: “There is a thing on the internet.
Scientists say that sitting still and breathing for fifteen minutes changes your brain. Have you heard of this?” Adunni
laughed. “I have been doing that since before you were born. We call it prayer.”
In Kyoto, Professor Yamamoto told his six graduate students: “The specification says 111.2 Hz. Our chamber
produces 109.3 Hz. The difference is the geology. Izu andesite is denser than Icelandic basalt. The frequency is lower
because the stone is heavier. This is not error. This is the earth being specific.” He published a note in the Journal of
the Acoustical Society of Japan. Title: “Local Geological Variation in Acoustic Resonance Chamber Output: An
Initial Observation.” Conclusion: “The specification is approximate. The body is approximate. Approximation is not
error.”
The earth was answering. Every geology had its own note. Every stone had its own song. The frequency was not a
number. It was a relationship between rock and air and the particular patient vibration of a planet that had been
humming to itself for 4.5 billion years.
